Q3：穩定幣與存款代幣皆由私部門發行，兩者有何差異？與 RWA 代幣化的關係？ 
A3：穩定幣是一種價值與美元或其他法幣掛勾的虛擬資產，存款代幣則是代幣化的銀行存款，兩者之
發行主體 、 價值基礎 、 取得方式及主要應用場景等皆不同 ， 惟均在區塊鏈或分散式帳本技術(DLT)
運作，均可支援 RWA 代幣之交易及結(清)算等金流作業，並可能在未來資產代幣化世界的貨幣
體系中扮演重要角色21。 
(一) 穩定幣與存款代幣皆由私部門發行，運用DLT 運作，惟兩者之發行主體、價值基礎、取得方式及
主要應用場景等皆不同 
1. 穩定幣在公共區塊鏈上運作22，價值與美元或其他法幣掛勾，由法幣資產擔保其價值，通常由非銀行部門發行，
屬該部門的負債，目前主要用途為虛擬資產市場的交易媒介，但應用範圍已逐漸擴及實體經濟的支付服務23。 
(1) 穩定幣常被用作洗錢的工具 ， 且可能因發行人的信用狀況、準備資產的透明度或安全性等因素，而有發行人倒閉、
準備資產不足或價值不穩定的風險，因此主要國家如美、日、歐及新加坡等均已著手予以納管，包括須以足額低
風險且高流動性資產作為支持，並有適當的資本、流動性與風險管理標準，讓穩定幣更加安全可靠。 
(2) 我國已由金管會研擬「虛擬資產服務法」草案24，其中亦包含穩定幣相關規範條文。 
2. 存款代幣為代幣化的銀行存款，屬商業銀行的負債，由於商業銀行受高度監理，安全性較穩定幣為高。 
(1) 在適法性方面，基於技術中立原則，國外機關如歐洲銀行監理機關(EBA)25及 BoE26認為，存款代幣係將現行存款

21  另可參見中央銀行(2025)，「資產代幣化世界之數位貨幣體系：存款代幣、穩定幣及央行數位貨幣(CBDC)扮演的角色」，央行理監事會後記者會參考資料，6
月 19 日。 
22  公共區塊鏈係指任何人皆可自由加入 、 查看交易紀錄並參與交易驗證的區塊鏈 ， 如以太坊(Ethereum)；私有區塊鏈則僅有經授權的成員才得以加入或驗證交易 。 
23  在部分新興國家，如高通膨或欠缺支付基礎設施的國家居民會將當地貨幣轉為美元穩定幣，當作美元使用並維持購買力外，其他用途也包括國際匯款、商業活
動及支付薪資等。Visa (2024), “Stablecoins: The Emerging Market Story,” Sep. 。 
24  該草案已於 2026 年 6 月 3 日經立法院初審通過。 
25  EBA (2024), “Report on Tokenised Deposits,” EBA/REP/2024/24. 
26  Bank of England (2024), “The Bank of England’s Approach to Innovation in Money and Payments,” Discussion Paper, Jul. 30.

### Page 101

98

之價值或權利，改以數位形式表示，不會因此改變存款人債權的經濟功能與基本性質，因此應適用銀行業之現行
規範與制度。 
(2) 在監管與風險控管機制方面，商業銀行受高度監理，須遵守嚴格的最低資本、流動性及風險管理等規範，具有存
款保險機制，必要時尚有央行可充當最後貸款者(last resort)，因此存款代幣之安全性較穩定幣為高。 
3. 存款代幣與穩定幣雖均為私部門發行之代幣化貨幣且於區塊鏈上運作，惟其取得方式及主要應用場景等具有差異
(見下頁表3)，兩者間可為互補、共存。 
(1) 由於存款代幣為代幣化的銀行存款，因此持有人須經商業銀行KYC 且開立帳戶後， 才能持有或使用存款代幣 ；穩
定幣因在公共區塊鏈上運作，且屬於可轉讓的債權，因此只要持有人能連接網路並透過數位錢包，即能取得或使
用穩定幣，對於部分面臨高通膨或資本管制較為嚴格的新興國家居民，相較於在銀行開立帳戶，更容易取得或使
用穩定幣。 
(2) 存款代幣與穩定幣均可支援可程式化及全天候(24/7)運作，主要應用場景差異如下： 
－ 存款代幣可供企業用於即時流動性管理或跨境支付等場景，部分大型銀行已積極進行相關研究及試驗。例如，美
國摩根大通銀行於 2019 年推出存款代幣 JPM Coin 供該行客戶於自行內部進行境內或跨境資金移轉27，並於本年
6 月協同花旗銀行、富國銀行、美國銀行等大型銀行及美國結算所公司(The Clearing House)宣布共建存款代幣網
路(金融聯盟鏈)28，以支援跨國企業於不同銀行間進行境內或境外之資金調度，提升跨境支付效率。 
－ 穩定幣目前主要作為虛擬資產交易媒介，並可用於零售匯款、支付或收受薪資，具有用於小額支付29或跨境支付潛
力。惟波士頓諮詢集團推估30，2024 年穩定幣主要用途仍為虛擬資產交易，比重達88%；至於實體經濟活動之支
付31僅占 6%，尚未廣泛應用。

(二)近年 RWA 代幣化規模持續成長，私部門發行之代幣化貨幣(如存款代幣、穩定幣)均可作為交易
與結(清)算工具32，但無論是使用存款代幣或穩定幣，涉及跨機構或跨體系之運作，仍需以央行貨
幣作為貨幣體系的清算資產及定錨 
1. 近年來RWA代幣化市場成長快速，多家機構預測未來將持續增長。 
(1) RWA 代幣化係將現實世界資產(包括股票、債券、基金等金融資產，黃金、房地產等實體資產)所衍生之經濟價
值及權利連結至區塊鏈上之代幣，使其可呈現於區塊鏈上，並於鏈上進行交易。 
(2) 近年來代幣化技術興起促成資產代幣化的發展，若能善加利用代幣化的技術優勢與所帶來的好處 ，如全天候、
跨境與跨時區交易、降低投資門檻、提高資產流動性等33，則可有效提升交易效率。因此，近年 RWA 代幣化市
場快速成長，總市值由 2023 年底之 21.6 億美元，成長至本年 5 月之 337.1 億美元(見下頁圖 6)，且許多機構樂
觀預測未來將持續大幅增長34。

32 例如零售投資人可持有存款代幣或穩定幣，並用以投資購買證券代幣，提升交易的共通性與效率。 
33 儘管將 RWA 代幣化具有許多好處，但仍有監管、法律等議題待釐清；此外，隨其規模日益擴大，亦須留意相關風險如傳遞衝擊、交易時間錯配及資訊揭露不
足等。參見中央銀行(2023)，「資產代幣化趨勢與未來貨幣體系發展願景：BIS 的觀點」，央行理監事會後記者會參考資料，12 月 14 日。 
34 例如麥肯錫顧問公司(McKinsey & Company)預估，至 2030 年，RWA 代幣市值將達 1.9 兆美元；渣打銀行預估，至 2028 年，RWA 代幣市值將達約 2 兆美元。

(3) 代幣化資產的世界仍需有代幣化的貨幣支援交易 、 交割及結(清)算等金流運作 ， 才能提高互通性 ， 增進交易效率 ；
由私部門發行的存款代幣或穩定幣，或將成為其中重要的交易與結(清)算工具。 
2.  即使存款代幣 、 穩定幣等可能在未來資產代幣化世界中扮演重要角色， 但最終仍需以央行貨幣作為最佳清算工具
及定錨，才能共同建構完善的數位貨幣體系。 
(1) 隨著資產代幣化的蓬勃發展，不同 DLT 平台或帳本之間需有共通的清算資產，使支付體系可支援任何代幣化資
產的清算服務，促進市場效率。 
(2) 央行貨幣由央行發行，無流動性風險與信用風險，即為最佳清算資產；在代幣化的世界中，需以代幣化的央行貨
幣35(即為批發型 CBDC 或為準備金代幣)作為跨體系、跨機構的最終清算資產，才能持續為代幣化的貨幣支付體
系提供信任基礎，並作為定錨。基此，本行將持續進行批發型 CBDC 相關試驗36，期建構未來代幣化貨幣體系基
礎設施，以確保貨幣支付之安全性、互通性與最終性。

35 為代幣化準備金，為批發型 CBDC 的一種，用於金融機構間代幣化資產的清算。 
36 2025 年起本行與集保結算所合作，試驗以批發型 CBDC 結合存款代幣，支援債券代幣採款券同步交割(DvP)之技術可行性。

Q4：穩定幣作為數位貨幣有何不足之處？ 
A4：穩定幣在公共區塊鏈上運作，惟受限於公共區塊鏈碎片化的傾向，不利貨幣的網路效應，加以不
符合單一性(singleness)、 彈性(elasticity)、 完整性(integrity)等三項貨幣的測試標準 ， 因此難以成為
貨幣體系的支柱，須透過妥適的監管確保其安全可靠，並以央行貨幣作為定錨。 
(一)公共區塊鏈有碎片化的傾向，不利穩定幣作為貨幣的網路效應 37 
1. 社會大眾願意接受某種貨幣，係因相信該貨幣未來亦能被他人接受，因此信任是貨幣體系的基礎38；當接受某種
貨幣的商家愈多，消費者採用該貨幣的誘因就愈高，從而形成正向循環，具有網路效應(network effect)，貨幣可
藉此協調經濟活動。 
2. 前BIS貨幣與經濟部門主管、現任南韓央行總裁申鉉松認為，穩定幣受限於公共區塊鏈碎片化的傾向，不利其作
為貨幣的網路效應。 
(1) 公共區塊鏈「去中心化」的設計，會導致許多不互通的區塊鏈相繼出現，形成碎片化現象。 
— 去中心化的設計係透過不同驗證者之間的共識，來取代對某一中心化機構的信任，亦即透過用戶交易活動之手續
費等獎勵作為誘因，鼓勵驗證者參與共識機制39。 
— 有許多用戶欲同時交易，區塊鏈將產生擁塞的情形，此時用戶須提高手續費，以使其交易優先受到處理，因而形
成交易活動愈多 、 手續費愈高的現象(見下頁圖7)；若費用過低 ，則可能無法提供足夠誘因吸引驗證者參與共識並
確保帳本安全性。 
— 當某一區塊鏈因交易活動增加而導致手續費大增，用戶基於成本考量，將分流至不互通的其他區塊鏈，例如對價
格敏感的用戶，將遷移到費用較低，但安全性或去中心化程度可能較弱之區塊鏈，形成碎片化現象(見下頁圖8)。

37 Shin, Hyun-Song (2026), “Tokenomics and Blockchain Fragmentation,” BIS Working Papers, No.1335, Mar. 
38 Carstens, Agustín (2022), “Digital Currencies and the Soul of Money,” Speech at the Goethe University's Institute for Law and  Finance (ILF) Conference, Jan. 18. 
39 例如，比特幣採用「工作量證明」(Proof of Work, PoW)作為共識機制，驗證者透過大量運算競爭解決密碼學難題，由最先解決者將交易紀錄打包成區塊，並獲
得代幣與手續費獎勵；以太坊(Ethereum)採用「權益證明」(Proof of Stake, PoS)，驗證者透過質押區塊鏈的原生代幣取得驗證資格，系統依質押比例選出負責確
認交易之驗證者，並由其他驗證者確認交易區塊，若有造假或惡意行為，質押代幣將被沒收。

### Page 106

103

(2) 穩定幣係在公共區塊鏈發行，故亦受限於公共區塊鏈碎片化的傾向，不利貨幣的網路效應。 
— 不同的區塊鏈間缺乏共同通訊機制，即使由同一發行人在不同區塊鏈上所發行的穩定幣亦無法直接互通。 
— 穩定幣跨鏈間的轉移須透過跨鏈橋(bridges)技術進行40，耗費時間與操作成本，且容易成為駭客的攻擊目標，此
外， 跨鏈 間缺乏穩定幣依 面值等價兌換 的信任基礎 ，因此 不同鏈上 的穩定幣 不具 無縫移 轉性質 (seamless 
transferability)。 
— 在不同區塊鏈上運作的穩定幣 ， 形同將用戶與流動性分散在不互通的孤島，造成流動性的碎片化(見下頁圖9)，損
害貨幣的網路效應。

40 在某一鏈上鎖定一種代幣，並在另一鏈上鑄造等值代幣的協議。

說明：圖中顯示不同區塊鏈上 DeFi 總鎖定價值(total value locked, TVL)之
占比，TVL 係鎖定於 DeFi 協議的虛擬資產總價值。

%

(二) 穩定幣不符合貨幣的三項測試標準 
BIS 認為41，從單一性、彈性、完整性等三項貨幣的測試標準來看，穩定幣均無法成為貨幣體系的支柱。 
1. 單一性：不同機構發行的貨幣，應能以面額等值支付及清算，方能成為使用者可無條件接受(no-questions-asked)
的支付工具。銀行間支付係由央行準備金作為交易的最終清算資產，實現支付的等值清算；穩定幣於次級市場交
易之兌換率則可能偏離面值，且不同穩定幣可能因準備資產、發行人信用等差異而產生折價或溢價。 
2. 彈性：指發行機構可依經濟體的需求，靈活調節貨幣發行量之能力。商業銀行貨幣(客戶存款)不必提存100%的準
備金，可在監管範圍內經由放貸而彈性擴張其資產負債表，滿足個人或企業借款需求；而穩定幣是採100%準備
的發行方式，如要增加供給，需要用戶提前支付法幣才能發行，發行人無法自行擴張穩定幣之供給。

41 BIS (2025), “The Next-Generation Monetary and Financial System,” BIS Annual Economic Report, Jun. 24.

1. 穩定幣在不同區塊鏈 (如乙太坊 、
Tron、Solana)上發行與流通，彼此
不互通，無法完全相互替代，不具
貨幣應有的無縫移轉性質。 
2. 穩定幣在不同區塊鏈上運作 ，將造
成流動性的碎片化，貨幣使用規模
難以集中，不利貨幣的網路效應。

### Page 108

105

3. 完整性：防止金融犯罪與非法活動的能力，以維持信任與穩定。 面對客戶的中介機構須實施嚴格 KYC程序及
AML/CFT等措施，客戶則須遵守相關規定以進行交易；而穩定幣屬於數位不記名支付工具42，可自由跨境流通、
出入不同交易所及自我託管錢包(self-hosted wallet)，在KYC合規上存在漏洞。 
(三) 為確保穩定幣的安全可靠，各國陸續著手納管穩定幣 
1. 為確保穩定幣的價值穩定 ，並降低被用作洗錢、詐欺等工具的風險 ，近來主要國家監理機關已陸續訂定規範
予以納管 。例如，美國訂定GENIUS法案，規範支付型穩定幣之初級市場發行相關事宜；歐盟則以MiCA規範虛
擬資產(含穩定幣)之初級市場發行及次級市場交易等相關事宜。 
2. 各國對穩定幣的規範項目 主要包括發行人資格 、準備資產管理 、贖回要求 、資訊揭露 、禁止向穩定幣持有人
支付利息 、須遵守洗錢防制 相關規範等。 
(四) 以央行貨幣為定錨係健全數位貨幣體系的關鍵 
1. 現代貨幣 體系由央行 提供信任基礎，建構貨幣的 網路效應， 使貨幣能充分發揮交易媒介 、計價單位及價值儲藏
等三大功能，促進經濟活動的協調。 
2. 依據前述三項貨幣的測試標準，BIS認為當前的雙層貨幣體系仍是最適模式 43，亦即以央行貨幣為核心 ，取代
成本較高且具有碎片化傾向的去中心化設計 ， 確保清算最終性及維護計價單位的穩定 ； 商業銀行及其他民間機構
則提供支持經濟活動所需的各項金融服務，並協助支付順暢。 
3. 未來數位貨幣體系仍須以央行貨幣為定錨，支援存款代幣或穩定幣的互通性及安全性；BIS建議 44，可建立 由央
行貨幣為核心的聯合帳本 (Unified Ledger)45，使不同銀行發行的 代幣化存款 及各類代幣化資產 共同存在一個
互通的平台，以 確保貨幣的網路效應及單一性 ，建構健全的數位貨幣體系。

42 穩定幣持有人可能並非發行人的客戶，發行人無法確認持有人是否已完成身分驗證。 
43 BIS (2025), “The Next-Generation Monetary and Financial System,” BIS Annual Economic Report, Jun. 24. 
44 BIS (2023), “Blueprint for the Future Monetary System: Improving the Old, Enabling the New,” BIS Annual Economic Report, Jun. 20. 
45 聯合帳本的架構選擇可視為一個連續光譜(continuum)，從極端的單一共享帳本到多個帳本透過橋接連接皆為可能的範圍，設計時可依需求進行選擇。

### Page 109

106

Q5：穩定幣對跨境資本移動及外匯市場之影響？ 
A5：穩定幣正逐步影響全球金融與支付體系 ， 其價格相對穩定 、 跨境移轉便捷及交易成本較低等特性 ，
有助提升跨境支付效率與資金流動性；惟其亦可能降低跨境資金流向透明度、加劇資本移動及匯
率波動，並影響資本流動管理措施之有效性。鑑於穩定幣對跨境資本流動、外匯市場及金融穩定
之潛在影響，IMF、BIS 及 FSB 均呼籲各國建立適當監理架構，強化風險管理與監督機制。 
(一) 穩定幣對跨境資本移動之影響 
1. 改變跨境資本移動模式，提升資金移動效率 
傳統跨境支付主要依賴 SWIFT 及代理銀行體系(correspondent banking system )，資金移轉通常需經過多層金融中
介，結算時間較長且交易成本較高。相較之下，穩定幣可透過區塊鏈網路進行點對點(P2P)移轉，得提供近乎即
時之跨境支付與結算服務，大幅降低交易及時間成本， 此一機制不僅有助提升國際貿易、跨境投資及跨境匯款之
效率 ， 亦有助於金融基礎設施尚不完善之新興市場與發展中地區提升金融服務可及性， 進一步強化普惠金融之發
展潛力。 
2. 降低跨境資金流動透明度，增加金融監理挑戰 
穩定幣具備跨境移轉快速、全天候交易、紀錄可追溯但持有人具匿名性(pseudonymity)等特性，加上其可透過境
外平台、自我託管錢包或點對點形式進行，使得相關交易較不受傳統金融監管46。此一去中介化流通特性，將降
低各國主管機關對資金來源、流向及最終用途的掌握度，形成監理資訊缺口(data gap)，增加洗錢防制、跨境資本
監測及金融監理挑戰。 
3. 提高跨境資本流動規模與波動性，影響資本流動管理之有效性 
穩定幣雖具有降低跨境支付成本及提升資金移動效率之潛力， 但亦可能擴大資本移動規模與波動性， 特別是在市

46 FATF (2026), “Targeted Report on Stablecoins and Unhosted Wallets – Peer-to-Peer Transactions,” Mar.

### Page 110

107

場面臨壓力或不確定性升高時，資金可快速轉換為美元穩定幣並跨境移轉，進一步加劇短期跨境資本流動，對於
資本帳尚未完全開放之經濟體而言，可能削弱既有資本流動管理措施(Capital Flow Management Measures, CFMs)
之有效性 ，甚至引發資本外流風險 。IMF 亦強調 ，各國主管機關應密切關注穩定幣及其他虛擬資產對資本流動穩
定性之影響，並防範其對既有資本流動管理措施所造成之潛在衝擊47。 
(二)穩定幣對外匯市場之影響48 
1. 形成銀行體系外之新型外匯交易渠道 
美元穩定幣雖於區塊鏈網路流通，但其價值仍錨定美元。當市場參與者以本國貨幣購買或贖回美元穩定幣時，實
質上已涉及本幣與美元間的兌換。隨著穩定幣交易規模逐漸擴大，部分原循傳統銀行體系進行的換匯需求，可能
轉移至虛擬資產服務商(VASP)，於銀行體系外形成新型態的外匯交易渠道。 
2. 穩定幣交易可能影響外匯市場供需 
美元穩定幣採 1:1 錨定美元機制，交易人以本幣買賣美元穩定幣，本質上仍涉及本幣與美元間的兌換。當此類需
求自銀行體系分流至虛擬資產服務商後 ，服務商為管理其庫存與匯率風險， 最終仍須透過銀行體系持有或調整美
元部位。因此，穩定幣交易所衍生之美元需求，最終將傳導至實體外匯市場，進而影響外匯市場之供需。 
3. 穩定幣交易可能加劇匯率波動 
IMF 及 BIS 研究指出，當美元穩定幣價格與銀行體系美元即期匯率出現價差時，將引發套利交易49；尤其於市場
波動劇烈期間，程式化高頻套利交易除可能推升外匯市場交易量外，亦可能加劇各國匯率波動。香港金融管理局
(HKMA)實證研究亦發現，新興市場貨幣與美元穩定幣交易量愈高，該貨幣之匯率波動度通常愈高50。

47 IMF (2023), “Elements of Effective Policies for Crypto Assets,” IMF Policy Paper, 2023/004, Feb.  
48 目前主要穩定幣價值係連結美元，因此本節皆以美元穩定幣分析說明。 
49 Aldasoro, I., Beltran, P., and Grinberg, F. (2026), “Stablecoin flows and spillovers to FX markets,” BIS Working Papers No. 1340 / IMF Working Paper WP/26/56, Mar.  
50 Hong Kong Monetary Authority (2026), “Assessing the Impact of Stablecoins on Exchange Rate Volatility: Evidence from Emerging  Market Economies,” Research 
Memorandum 02/2026, Feb. 24.

### Page 111

108

4. 穩定幣脫鉤風險可能衝擊外匯市場穩定 
若美元穩定幣因發行機構信用風險 、 儲備資產品質疑慮或市場信心惡化而發生擠兌或脫鉤(depeg)事件 ， 虛擬資產
服務商可能需於短時間內大幅調整外匯部位 ， 此類風險事件可能透過套利交易 、 流動性管理及避險操作等管道 ，
將虛擬資產市場風險傳導至外匯市場51，加劇匯率波動並影響市場穩定。 
(三)穩定幣對資本移動與外匯管理之挑戰 
穩定幣之應用可能使部分跨境資金流動逐步脫離既有外匯管理架構，除增加主管機關掌握跨境資本流向之難度，
並可能削弱資本移動及外匯管理措施之有效性。因此，各國已逐漸將穩定幣納入總體審慎監理及外匯管理之重要政
策議題。 
1. 國際組織之監理倡議 
面對穩定幣所帶來之新興風險，國際重要金融組織已陸續提出監理原則與政策建議。其中，IMF 與 FSB 指出，
主管機關應辨識並妥善因應穩定幣及其他虛擬資產對總體經濟及金融穩定之影響，包括要求虛擬資產服務商申
報相關資料，以縮小資訊缺口，並透過國際合作及資訊交換機制提升監理效能52。BIS 亦建議，各國應借鏡傳統
金融監理經驗，依據穩定幣之功能與風險特性，建立相應之監理架構53。 
2. 本行之監理立場與因應 
在此國際監理趨勢下，為維持外匯管理制度之一致性與有效性，本行將於 「虛擬資產服務法」 授權架構下，依循
「相同活動、相同風險、相同監管(same activity, same risk, same regulation)」原則，建立穩定幣外匯管理機制，
維護我國外匯市場秩序與穩定。

51 Aldasoro, I., Beltran, P., and Grinberg, F. (2026), “Stablecoin flows and spillovers to FX markets,” BIS Working Papers No. 1340 / IMF Working Paper WP/26/56, Mar.  
52 IMF-FSB (2023), “Synthesis Paper: Policies for Crypto-Assets,” IMF-FSB Joint Paper, Sep. 7.  
53 Iñaki Aldasoro , Matteo Aquilina , Ulf Lewrick  and Sang Hyuk Lim  (2025), “Stablecoin growth – policy challenges and approaches,” BIS Bulletin  No. 108, Bank for 
International Settlements, Jul. 11.

### Page 112

109

(1) 建立穩定幣移轉資料通報機制 
針對境內虛擬資產服務商提供之穩定幣移轉服務，規劃建立資料通報制度，以強化對鏈上資金流向、跨境移轉及
交易規模之掌握，提升跨境資本移動之監理能力。 
(2) 穩定幣交易納入既有外匯管理架構 
將穩定幣交易納入既有外匯管理架構 ， 以確保不同金融通路間適用相同監理標準 ， 避免因業務型態轉換而產生監
理套利空間，維護外匯管理制度之一致性與完整性。 
(3) 持續關注穩定幣市場發展，審慎因應相關風險 
本行將持續關注穩定幣市場發展，包括交易規模、價格偏離情形、跨境資金流向及虛擬資產服務商業務模式等，
並透過資料通報制度、鏈上監理科技及外匯管理規範之完善，持續強化監理量能，維護我國外匯市場秩序及匯率
穩定。

MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

內外皆美

調升投資評等至買進，目標價 82 元，上漲空間 49%。 
1. 3Q26 毛利率突破 20%，EPS1.34 元，優於預期。 
2. 漲價及台幣貶值雙重有利因子加持，估計 4Q26 季增雙位數。 
3. 泰國新廠新增 68.23 億泰銖投資案。

3Q26 毛利率突破 20%，EPS1.34 元，優於預期 
3Q26 合併營收 48.12 億元，QOQ+13.5%，YOY+24.7%， ，毛利率20.5%，較前
一季 12.2%及同期 8.6%分別大增 8.3 及 11.9ppt，大幅優於市場共識，而毛利率提
升主要是順利反應原料成本及產品組合優化等，另匯兌利益挹注近一億元，單季稅
後淨利 5.33 億元，QOQ+196.6%、YOY+617.9%，EPS 1.34 元，表現優於預期。
累計前三季稅後淨利 9.6 億元，YOY+152.6%，EPS 2.42 元。

漲價及台幣貶值雙重有利因子加持，估計 4Q26 季增雙位數 
展望 4Q26，AI 產能供應不求外溢效益持續顯現，加上原料緊缺，下游車用客戶
漲價接受度提升，公司表示目前訂單需求遠大於供給，而營收及成長力道視
CCL、PP、鑽針..等原料供應狀況，現在主要瓶頸就是缺料。考量產品價格調漲及
近期台幣貶值有利因子，富邦估計季營收成長 16.5%，毛利率向上，單季
EPS1.73 元，QOQ+29.3%，YOY+171.2%。

泰國新廠新增 68.23 億元泰銖投資案 
泰國新廠預計 2026 年底前完工，預計 1H27 產出貢獻，公司表示該廠非單一客戶
專廠，產品應用也非單一。另因應未來營運發展需求，公司近日泰國廠董事會也通
過 68.23 億元泰銖投資案 ，預計 2026 年到 2029 年進行產能擴充的設備購置計劃。

富邦不同之處 
富邦為少數持續追蹤且出具更新報告券商之一。

評價與風險 
季報表現大幅優於市場預期，展望後市，受惠價格調漲順利及台幣貶值效益，營運
獲利向上，以 2027 年的 8.1 倍 P/E 評價來看並不高，因此我們調升投資評等至買
進，目標價 82 元，約相當 2027 年的 12 倍 P/E。

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition 
Buy Expected absolute return to be over 15% within the next 6 months  
Neutral  Expected absolute return to be level within the next 6 months  
Sell  Expected absolute return to be over negative 15% within the next 6 months 
Not Rated (NR)  Pursuant to Fubon acting in deals involving the company or there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an investment rating  
Under Review Fubon is in the process of determining an investment rating and will assign a rating within the 3- to 6-month horizon

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months 
Market Weight  Sector expected to perform in line with the broader market averages within the next 6-12 months 
Underweight  Sector expected to underperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months.

### Page 1

Wiwynn

AI-driven sales growth since 4Q; reiterate 
Buy and trim PO to NT$2,310

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 2,310 TWD | Price: 1,555 TWD

3Q beat driven by both general and ASIC servers 
Wiwynn’s 3Q sales grew 56% QoQ/63% YoY, supported by sustained general server 
demand as well as a strong ramp of ASIC servers (e.g., AWS Trainium 3) from Sep. 
GM/OPM of 8.87%/7.47% was above BofAe of 7.77%/6.42% and consensus of 
8.42%/6.85%. OP was 40%/16% above BofAe/consensus. After a non-op. loss (mainly 
from higher interest expenses), NP was 34%/10% above BofAe/consensus.  
4Q sales led by ASIC; GPU sales expected by year-end 
The company posted strong Oct sales of NT$165.1bn (-4% MoM/+81% YoY), tracking 
35%/36% of BofA/consensus estimates. Its general server business was impacted by 
component shortages in Oct, despite demand remaining strong. For AI servers, we 
expect ASIC to be the major sales driver in 4Q with a strong ramp since Sep; while GPU 
servers (from multiple customers) are expected to deliver sales contribution by year-end.  
Solid sales growth in 2027-28; GM pressured by AI  
Our checks indicate that Wiwynn’s ASIC projects remain on a buy-and-sell model, 
although a consignment business model is under discussion; while business models for 
GPU projects have yet to be confirmed. With strong AI server momentum, we expect its 
GM to decline QoQ to 7.67% in 4Q26. For 2027/28, we model solid YoY sales growth of 
+60%/+31% , with conservative GMs of 6.67%/6.51% in 2027/28, vs. 8.30% in 2026E.  
Reiterate Buy; trim PO to NT$2,310 
We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 4-14%, mainly to reflect the 3Q26 beat and solid sales 
growth aided by general and ASIC server projects. We however trim PO to NT$2,310 
(15x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS) from NT$2,387 (18x 2H26-1H27E EPS), as we roll over our 
valuation base and lower multiple on macro uncertainties and concerns over margin 
decline. Reit. Buy on robust ASIC momentum and a constructive general server outlook.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
Wiwynn supplies server, storage and cloud equipment to 
global cloud service providers (CSPs). 100% of its sales 
comes from server/datacenter business. Key clients are 
Meta, Microsoft, and Amazon. Wiwynn was founded in April 
2012 and was listed on Taiwan Stock Exchange on 27 
March 2019. As of 2Q26, Wistron (3231 TT) owned 35.27% 
of shares in Wiwynn. Besides the headquarter in Taiwan, its 
subsidiaries and operations are located in Malaysia, Hong 
Kong, China, South Korea, Japan, the US and Mexico.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Wiwynn as we are positive on 
increasing contribution from AI server projects, delivery in 
Amazon after efforts for years, and regular server 
bottoming out from 2H23, driving double-digit 
sales/earnings growth in 2026-28E.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
Wiwynn (XWIQF) 
Our PO of NT$2,310 is based on 15x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS, which is 1.5-2.0SD above 
Wiwynn's historical P/E average since listing. We believe such a multiple is reasonable to 
reflect Wiwynn's rising sales contribution from AI server, delivery in Amazon projects, 
and regular server demand bottoming out from 2H23.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) higher-than-expected capex growth from key clients, (2) 
stronger-than-expected momentum in server/datacenter infrastructure, (3) more project 
wins/launches for AI server, and (4) much higher contribution from the third largest 
customer.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) unexpected slowdown of key clients' capex spending in 
server/datacenter, (2) more intensified competition from peers, and (3) weaker 
momentum in AI server projects.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

## Document 190: 261112_citi_chailease

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_citi_chailease.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

### Page 1

Better Interest Spread — Group interest spread widened 8bps qoq to 6.95%, 
mainly driven by better China spread. China spread improved 19bps qoq, as loan 
yield increased 8bps qoq and funding cost was 11bps lower qoq. Taiwan loan spread 
narrowed 7bps qoq, primarily due to 6bps qoq higher funding cost amid Fed rate 
hike and tightening domestic liquidity. 
Muted Loan Growth — Group loans grew by 1% qoq/4% yoy, mainly driven by 
ASEAN portfolio growth and RMB appreciation. Taiwan portfolio was flat qoq/-1% 
yoy. China portfolio was +2% qoq/flat yoy in local currency (+3% qoq/+11% yoy in 
NTD). Management suggested that the China business has achieved positive loan 
growth in the past three months and they expected positive momentum ahead. 
ASEAN portfolio was+2% qoq/+10% yoy,  
Broadly Stable China Asset Quality, Higher ASEAN Credit Cost — Group 
delinquency ratio slightly increased 3bps qoq to 5.16%. Taiwan delinquency ratio 
increased 20bps qoq, while NPL formation improved qoq. China delinquency ratio 
was stable qoq, but NPL formation slightly picked up qoq per our estimates. Group 
credit cost was 2.49%, +11bps qoq, mainly due to higher ASEAN credit costs on the 
back of macro uncertainties especially in Cambodia. 
Implications — Despite steady profit growth in 10M26 and stabilizing China asset 
quality, Chailease shares continued to de-rate this year amid concerns over growth. 
Management sounded more upbeat on China loan growth outlook at the results 
briefing, and the impact from auto financing business on Taiwan loan growth should 
gradually fade. FY26E dividend yield of 6% is attractive, in our view.

Chailease Holding (5871.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
2
Chailease Holding
Valuation
Our target price for Chailease of NT$130 is based on 1.2x 2026E P/B, using a Gordon Growth model. Our valuation
methodology takes into account a sustainable ROE estimate of 12.1%, a cost-of-equity assumption of 11.1%, and a long-term
growth rate of 6.0%. We use a blended approach to calculate both risk-free rates and long-term growth rates taking into
account local estimates on these parameters in the major markets in which Chailease operates, namely Taiwan, China, and

Thailand, and weighted them by asset mix.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the Chailease stock from reaching our target price are: [1] slower-than-expected asset
growth momentum; [2] asset quality deterioration in different markets; [3] higher operating expenses, which drag bottom-line
growth; [4] earlier-than-expected capital-raising that dilutes ROE and BVPS; and [5] ASEAN expansion that is slow in
contributing to group earnings. Key upsides risks to our target price include: [1] faster-than-expected asset quality recovery as

macro uncertainties ease; [2] higher-than-expected asset growth momentum.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV
Prepared for Kevin Lu

catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 195: 261112_daiwa_HH

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_daiwa_HH.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Hon Hai’s 3Q26 results, announced 12 November. 
We are impressed with its opex control, which led to solid operating margin 
expansion despite gross margin dilution from rising AI server contribution.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat. Net income was TWD78bn (EPS: 
TWD5.53), 25% higher than our estimate (TWD62bn) and 10% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus (TWD70bn). Revenue was TWD3.0tn (+19% 
QoQ; +46% YoY) with better-than-expected smartphone contribution. The 
gross margin came in at 6.02% (vs. 6.12% in 2Q26), a 0.1pp decline QoQ 
on a rising cloud segment mix. That said, the operating margin expanded 
0.07pp to 3.83%, driven by strong opex control and better leverage.

Key updates: Hon Hai reaffirms its positive view of its 2027 revenue 
momentum, targeting double-digit growth YoY (similar to our forecast). The 
company believes its cloud business will remain a key growth driver (54% 
of total revenue in 3Q26). Regarding total AI server racks, Hon Hai expects 
to see high-double-digit growth in 2027 (vs. our expectation of c.30% YoY 
growth from GPU server plus a rising ASIC contribution; however, as ASIC 
AI servers typically utilise consignment models, its AI server revenue 
growth will be slightly diluted). As for smartphones, considering Hon Hai’s 
key clients’ models command high ASPs, we believe the impact from rising 
memory costs will be relatively smaller, but we expect the PC segment to 
decline YoY due to the negative impact from rising component costs.

During the 3Q26 results call, AI server end demand from the US was the 
hottest question, especially after the US mid-term elections. We believe 
management’s view on hyperscalers’ and neocloud’s near-term demand 
remains positive, but the long-term impact must be closely monitored. Also, 
as investors are worried about the AI server capex slowdown from CSPs, 
management also indicated that there would be new end demand from 
sovereign and enterprise groups, which could act as a second growth 
wave. For 4Q26, the company is guiding for >15% revenue growth QoQ 
and YoY given the ICT peak season along with rising AI server revenue (up 
by 5-15% QoQ due to ongoing AI server shipment strength).

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 10-12% to reflect 
higher ASP assumptions for AI servers. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating but 
trim the 12M TP to TWD320, using a lower target PER of 13x (prior 16x; vs. 
its past-3-year range of 8-18x) on our 1-year forward EPS, given the overall 
weaker sentiment. Downside risk: low demand for AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E revenue are above the consensus by 2-3%, 
likely as we are more optimistic on Hon Hai’s AI server shipments.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: solid opex control

➢ Reiterating Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD320

Company profile 
Established in 1974, Hon Hai Precision Industry (Hon Hai) started out making plastic products, 
connector products and cable assemblies. It is now the largest electronics manufacturing service (EMS) 
provider globally in terms of design, manufacturing, global logistics and after-market service.

### Page 5

Hon Hai Precision Industry (2317 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Hon Hai has incorporated 4 independent members into its 7-member board of directors in pursuit
of better governance. However, the company's Chairman also serves concurrently as President,
implying an absence of separation between board leadership and executive management.
Capital management 1 Hon Hai has declared >40% payout ratio for most of the years since 2014, and >50% in the past 5 years, pointing to a shareholder-friendly dividend policy.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related parties accounted for 1.6% of total revenue for 2025, while purchases
from related parties constituted 0.1% of cost of sales. We believe there is limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
S Labor practices 2
Hon Hai recognises employees as the most important asset and continues to invest in its
workforce. In terms of safety, Hon Hai has upgraded its occupational safety and health
management system since 2019, and obtained the ISO45001 and CNS45001 certificates. The
company held online activities related to factory safety and health. Overall, we note that Hon Hai
has improved in terms of labour management in recent years. Hon Hai established a team to
implement data security management. Moreover, it plans to conduct data security training and
education regularly for all employees. Hon Hai aims to achieve zero data security incident in the
future.
S Product design & lifecycle management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
Company specific Management Analyst comments
E GHG emissions 2
In November 2020, Hon Hai announced its commitment to achieving net zero greenhouse gas
emissions by 2050. Its Scope 1 and 2 GHG emissions decreased by 53.6% in 2025 compared
with 2020, exceeding its reduction target of 21%. That said, the company's actual energy-saving
rate in mainland China was 5.2% in 2025, successfully achieving its energy-saving goal of 4.2%.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

All Ring Tech Co. (6187.TWO): CoWoS expansion to drive growth ahead; 
Oct rev on track; 3Q26 OP income in line; Buy
12 November 2026 | 7:57AM HKT
Oct revenues on track: Oct revenues came in at NT$978mn, +218% YoY but -16% 
MoM (vs. 3Q26 revenues +41% QoQ), accounting for 33% of our 4Q26E revenue 
forecast. Looking into 4Q26, we expect revenue to decline 11% QoQ, yet 24% ahead 
of our prior forecast on stronger-than-expected CoWoS related demand, while we 
lower our GM assumption to 50.6% (vs. 52.6% previously) to reflect a less favorable 
product mix and early stage shipments of new tools for panel level packaging and 
CPO. For 2027, we expect continued CoWoS capacity expansion at both foundry and 
OSAT customers, rising content value in panel level packaging and the ramp of the 
new CPO product line to drive growth, with GM set to recover as new products reach 
scale. Maintain Buy.

3Q26 operating income in line: Revenue was pre-announced. GM of 48.5% came in 
below GSe/Bloomberg consensus of 54.9%/54.3%, which we attribute to product 
mix, with a higher contribution from lower-margin automation equipment. However, 
with a larger revenue scale and a lower opex ratio of 15.6% (vs. GSe/cons. of 
18.2%/17.7%), operating income grew 39% QoQ, 2% / 1% behind GSe/cons., largely 
in line. Below the operating line, All Ring booked a non-operating loss of NT$55mn in 
3Q26 (vs. non-operating income of NT$174mn in 2Q26), primarily attributable to 
valuation losses from equity investment, partly reversing the gains booked in 2Q26, 
which led EPS (NT$8.49) to miss by 10% vs GSe.

### Page 2

CoWoS expansion and new product ramps to drive growth: We expect 4Q26E 
revenue to decline 11% QoQ, still well ahead of our prior forecast on CoWoS-related 
demand. Our forecast implies average monthly revenue of NT$983mn in 
November-December (broadly flat vs. October). On the margin front, we expect GM to 
remain subject to product mix, with new tools carrying lower margins in the initial ramp. 
Longer term, we remain positive on All Ring, and expect 2027-28E growth to be driven 
by 1) continued CoWoS capacity expansion at both foundry and OSAT customers; 2) 
panel-level packaging, which brings higher-ASP tools and additional process steps, 
lifting content value per line; 3) the ramp of its new CPO product line, spanning fiber 
coupling and optical engine inspection tools, which we expect to start contributing 
meaningfully in 2027; and 4) new customer penetration, including a US customer’s 
advanced packaging capacity.

Earnings revision: We incorporate the 3Q26 results into our model and revise our 
2026/2027/2028E EPS by +2.7%/0.0%/+1.7%, as 1) higher revenue 
(+9.4%/+1.4%/+3.6% vs. our prior forecasts) on stronger CoWoS-related demand and 2) 
a lower opex ratio on the larger revenue base are offset by 3) lower GM, reflecting the 
product mix and the initial ramp of new products, and 4) the non-operating loss in 
3Q26. Net net, we expect All Ring’s revenue to grow 87%/33%/29% YoY in 2026-28E 
(vs. 71%/44%/26% previously), with GM of 51.0%/53.1%/53.5% (vs. 
53.8%/54.2%/54.9% previously).

Valuation: We maintain our 12-month TP at NT$1,450 (implied 35x 2027E PE vs. 36% 
NI YoY of 2027-28E on avg.), still based on a target P/E multiple of 35x (unchanged; 
derived by applying the implied earnings growth uplift (per GSe) to the company’s 
historical valuation framework) applied to our FY27E (unchanged) EPS. We view CPO as 
an even stronger structural tailwind for All Ring than CoWoS, with the potential to drive 
multiple-fold earnings expansion over the next several years, further supporting both 
earnings visibility and scope for valuation re-rating. Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

## Document 217: 4555氣立 第一金

### Page 1

羅子評 
(886-2)2563-1666 
jason.luo@firstsec.com.tw

氣立(4555) 
區間, TP 55 (潛在報酬 7.84%)  
區間 
Price(08/13)：51 
Target Price：55

氣立受惠於 AI 基礎建設與中國自動化產業需求強勁 ， 浙江平湖廠
產能持續滿載，且精密金屬加工訂單能見度維持  2 至 3 個月水
準 ， 本業營運已步入正軌 。 公司藉由集中上海廠生產與產線優化有
效控管成本，26Q2 毛利率成長至 32.95%，EPS 達 0.65 元，同時
積極將產品觸角延伸至 半導體廠務與  AI 伺服器液冷散熱等領
域，向智慧製造解決方案轉型，新業務效益正逐步顯現。在稼動率
維持高檔及新世代產品導入的加持下，研究部預估公司  2026 年
全年營收達 18.62 億元，年增 32.21%，EPS 有望提升至 1.46 
元。 
評價方面，氣立過往五年 PBR 區間落在 0.8x 至 1.7x，目前評
價約 1.2x PBR，位於歷史中緣偏上。考量整體市場回溫、平湖廠
新產能持續擴張 ， 加上新產品營收貢獻逐步發酵 ， 研究部將評價調
升至過往 5 年中緣之 1.3x PBR，設定目標價 55 元，投資建議為
區間操作。   
⚫ 近期投資評等 ⚫ 公司基本資料 
氣立主要從事氣動元件之研發、製造及銷售等業務，營運模式
以 OEM 為主，同時以CHELIC 品牌行銷全球市場。同時經營
金屬加工業務。生產基地橫跨泰山廠、大陸上海松江廠及浙江
平湖廠，以及泰國廠。

⚫ 生產優化，26Q2 毛利率年季雙增至 32.95%，EPS 0.65 元 
公司 26Q2 營收 4.92 億，QoQ 21.19%，YoY 16.58%；其中
氣動元件佔比約 6 成 ， 精密金屬加工業務佔比近4 成 ， 同時兩
業務皆呈現雙位數成長；透過氣動元件集中至上海生，同時透
過規模採購與生產流程優化，有效降低成本， 並於精密製造導
入自動化提良率。毛利率攀升至 32.95%，季增 2.82 個百分
點，年增 0.87 個百分點；因營收與毛利同步提升，帶動營業
利益達 0.36 億，QoQ 333.28% ，YoY 81.30%；業外受惠處
分旗下子公司認列 0.19 億元利益，稅後獲利達0.45 億，QoQ 
1691.81%，年轉盈；EPS 為 0.65 元，本業約 0.44 元，業
外約 0.21 元。

⚫ 新事業轉型效益浮現，估 26 年 EPS 1.46 元，營收年增 32% 
研究部對氣立後市營運持正向態度，主因(1)受惠 AI 與中國自

除息日 2026/06/02 動化產業需求延續， 帶動旗下氣動元件與精密金屬加工業務持
續成長 ，(2) 稼動率維持高位 ， 精密金屬加工訂單能見度達2-3
個月水準。 
平湖廠持續滿載，訂單能見度維持 2-3 個月水準 
產能利用率方面，上海廠稼動率穩居 70%～80% 水準，主要
係因公司策略性將傳統氣動元件集中至該廠生產 ， 並藉由規模
採購與生產流程重塑 （降低零件總數、精簡組裝工序） 有效提
升生產效益。另一方面，受惠於 AI 基礎建設帶動，以及中國
本土 PCB 相關生產設備需求強勁，浙江平湖廠自 25H1 以
來產能持續滿載。為因應強勁訂單，公司已於  2025 年底擴
增一條新生產線，目前亦持續進行設備升級與製程優化，預期
將進一步增添營運動能。 
新品導入，長期挹注營收成長與獲利優化 
目前氣立正積極推動營運轉型，將業務聚焦於感測、電控、氣
動、真空及精密製造等五大產品線。其中，主力新品「超音波
流量感測器」搭配電動球閥設計，可同步監控壓力、溫度、流
量及流速，並具備自動調節流量之功能。該產品主要應用於半
導體廠務系統，目前正積極爭取 AI 伺服器之液冷散熱認證，
並將應用觸角延伸至水資源 、 生技製藥及自動化設備等多元產
業；自 26Q2 末起，相關產品已從樣品測試階段，順利轉為
小量訂單正式出貨。此外，新一代「五指靈巧手」亦由原先的
三指夾爪，全面升級為五指獨立運作之機構設計。

展望後市，氣立的新業務佈局與轉型效益已逐步顯現。藉由近
年來的產品集中生產與產線優化，搭配穩定挹注佔營收  6% 
至 8% 的研發量能，公司已成功由傳統氣動元件供應商，轉
型為智慧製造解決方案提供商。研究部預期 2026 年在稼動率
維持高檔、成本控管得宜及新世代產品導入加持下，預估公司
2026 全年營收為 18.62 億，YoY 16.17%； 毛利率 32.21% ，
年增 1.31 個百分點；營業利益 1.11 億，YoY 126.49%；稅
後獲利 1.02 億，YoY 655.16%；EPS 為 1.46 元；每股淨值
約 42.53 元。

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 241: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340원에서 1,315원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026년 예상 FCF의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

2026-10-29

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

## Document 279: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82만원에서 64만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

## Document 296: AI交易重燃，美股指反弹，CoreWeave飙升24%，美元续涨，黄金跌破4000

周三纳斯达克 100 指数上涨 2.3% ，创 5 ⽉以来最佳单周表现；标普涨 1.3% ，道指涨 0.5% 。
美国中期选举后，美股主要指数均转涨。英伟达上涨 2.87% ，盘后解职传闻后、股价暴跌
超 8% 。特斯拉上涨约 3% ；⽽亚⻢逊、 Meta 和微软则各下跌约 2% 。美元指数创下 2025 年 5

美国中期选举后，美股主要指数均转涨。周三纳斯达克 100 指数上涨 2.3% ，创 5 ⽉以来最佳单周
表现；标普 500 指数上涨 1.3% ，道琼斯⼯业平均指数上涨 0.5% 。
此次反弹有效缓解了市场此前对 AI 投资主题降温的忧虑。 Miller Tabak 的 Matt Maley 表⽰，科技
股的强势回升打消了外界对 Anthropic IPO 能否顺利完成的疑问。若 IPO 受阻，将引发外界对 "AI

泡沫 " 是否破裂的质疑。
与此同时，⾼盛交易台数据显⽰，对冲基⾦主导了当⽇⼤规模买盘，其AI 相关篮⼦指数单⽇涨
幅达 5% ，重新收复⼤选前⽔平。英伟达交易时段收涨 2.87% ，盘后解职传闻后、股价暴跌超
8% 。
然⽽，⻛险情绪回暖的背⾯是宏观压⼒的持续积聚。通胀数据令市场加息预期升温，美元指数
攀升⾄ 2025 年 5 ⽉以来⾼位，10 年期美债期货隐含收益率重新突破 5% 。⻩⾦时隔四个交易⽇，
重新跌破 4000 美元下⽅。
彭博宏观策略师 Frank Monkam 指出，本轮反弹在跨资产波动率居⾼不下的宏观环境下显得较为
脆弱，整体隐含和实现波动率均未回落⾄此前低位，跨资产波动率交叉传导的⻛险依然不容忽
视。

周三美股三⼤指数均收涨。标普 500 指数成功重回 100 ⽇均线上⽅。

势，仅三只股票就占据了全市场成交量的 32%。

云计算巨头（即 " 付钱⽅ " ）。

指数的表现仍然逊于标普 500 指数 493 。

### Page 5

在 " 七巨头 " 内部也出现分化：英伟达上涨超 2.87% ，特斯拉上涨约 3% ；⽽亚⻢逊、 Meta 和微软
则各下跌约 2% 。
多⽅看好 AI 主线，但分歧犹存
机构观点整体倾向乐观，但对⾏情持续性判断不⼀。
Nationwide 的 Mark Hackett 表⽰，过去⼀周堪称市场叙事快速切换的典型案例。他认为，尽管投
资者关注 AI 前景不确定性和通胀压⼒，但强劲宏观背景、历史性盈利增⻓、季节性顺⻛以及预期
与估值的充分重置，构成了市场的重要⽀撑。 " 若怀疑情绪降温，多头将获得明显优势。 "
Edward Jones 的 Brock Weimer 认为， AI 投资主线依然完整，但随着基础设施建设趋于成熟，未
来⼀年主要参与者的资本⽀出和盈利增速可能放缓。
他表⽰，其公司继续看好美国⼤盘股，认为这类资产以低于 10 年平均远期市盈率的⽔平提供了
颇具吸引⼒的 AI 敞⼝，同时也看好可受益于经济强劲的中盘股。
对于 CoreWeave 本⾝，市场存在明显分歧。
多⽅认为，其以客⼾合同⽀撑的融资模式具有⾃洽性：每份合同单独融资，债务在合同期内全

额偿还， GPU 届时归 CoreWeave 所有；随着装机规模扩⼤，⾃由现⾦流有望在 2030 年转正（⾼
盛预测）。

### Page 6

空⽅则指出，管理层引述的每兆⽡ 20 ⾄ 25 百万美元定价与财报所反映的 9 ⾄ 13 百万美元存在明显
差距，明年融资缺⼝约为 330 亿美元，且 2028 年前将有约 55 吉⽡新供给⼊市，约为 CoreWeave
现有规模的 20 倍，届时旧 GPU 租⾦将承压，⽽ 470 亿美元债务需在芯⽚贬值前悉数偿还。
宏观压⼒未退，美元强势、⻩⾦承压
在美国退伍军⼈节假⽇导致国债现货市场休市的背景下，债市期货⼩幅⾛低， 10 年期美债期货
隐含收益率重新站上 5% 。联邦基⾦期货显⽰，市场已充分定价 2027 年两次 25 个基点的加息，前
端加息预期当⽇再度温和上⾏。
美元应声⾛强，彭博美元现货指数上涨 0.4% ，欧元下跌 0.7% ⾄ 1.1088 美元，⽇元下跌 0.7% ⾄
每美元 159.87 ，美元指数创下 2025 年 5 ⽉以来最⾼⽔平，并录得 6 ⽉以来两⽇最⼤涨幅。
⾦价承压回落，现货⻩⾦下跌 1.2% ⾄每盎司 3984.37 美元，重新跌破 4000 美元关⼝。

⽐特币随科技股⾛强，反弹 1.1% ⾄ 74581 美元，收复此前跌⾄三个⽉低点的失地。

## Document 366: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

## Document 384: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP , household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP . The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP , is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

## Document 418: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors ( see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non -AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers 1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to mome ntum that is both solid and broad -based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

## Document 442: Time to find 2027 soaring stocks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Time to find 2027 soaring stocks.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    陸⾏之  Andrew Lu on global semis and techs <andrewhclu@substack.com>

stocks

利有倍增潛⼒的公司都會在獲利還沒爆發前提前先跑，⽽造成本益⽐
